Text:"Focused Omni-Modal GUI Agents Fuyao Zhang1, Wenwen He1, Jiaming Zhang1, Yurong Hao1, Xuan Wang1, Guowei Guan1, Che Wang1, Longtao Huang2, Wei Yang Bryan Lim1 1Nanyang Technological University, 2Alibaba Group GitHub (cid:128) Homepage Abstract Graphical User Interface (GUI) agents increasingly act over streaming omni-modal observations spanning screenshots, audio, video, interaction history, and device states. However, exposing this complete context uniformly at every step ignores that relevance is transient and sparse: the signal determining one action can become clutter at the next. Because executed actions feed back into the context, these errors compound into Sequential Distraction, driving the agent into irreversible trajectory drift. We formulate the underlying requirement as Focused Perception: representation that is step-adaptive, action-sufficient, and non-intrusive. We realize this in FocusGUI, lightweight adapter that dynamically prioritizes multimodal channels against step-specific decision reference. Instead of uniformly processing all inputs, it compresses the most relevant evidence into compact latent and subtly injects it into the frozen decoders embeddings, guiding the models attention without altering its native parameter space. FocusGUI trains from action supervision alone, requiring neither modality-relevance labels nor backbone fine-tuning. Across four omni-modal backbones, it raises overall Type Match by +33.2 and Exact Match by 3.0 over the frozen backbone on average, and Type Match by +30.1 over the strongest existing enhancement method. Text:"OmniAI Group of ZJU ACES Lab VLA-Rolling: Fewer Denoising Steps, Better Control via Adaptive Action Reuse Sicheng He1, Yi Pan1, Qiuting Yu1, Jiaming Huang1, Qi Lu1, Shuyan Wu1, Hongyan Feng1, Xuanyu Liu1, Jun Zhang1, Jie Zhang1, Wenqi Zhang1, Xuhong Zhang1, Jianwei Yin1 1Zhejiang University Flow-matching vision-language-action (VLA) models generate an action chunk by iteratively denoising Gaussian noise, but typically execute only short prefix before replanning with updated observations. Under this receding-horizon paradigm, the unexecuted remainderthe action tailis discarded, and the next chunk is regenerated from fresh noise. We show that this discarded tail retains transferable denoising progress: action-tail initialization can match or outperform native 10-step Gaussian baseline using fewer denoising steps. However, the optimal denoising budget varies substantially across samples, indicating that tail reuse should be adapted to the updated environment. Based on this observation, we introduce VLA-Rolling, lightweight inference framework that reuses action tails through three stages: action-tail inheritance, per-action confidence estimation, and confidenceguided refinement through renoising and denoising budget allocation, while the underlying VLA remains frozen. Across π0.5, SmolVLA, X-VLA, and MolmoAct2 on LIBERO, MetaWorld, LIBEROPlus, and RoboTwin 2.0, VLA-Rolling reduces action-chunk inference cost in all 16 modelbenchmark pairs, achieving up to 1.48 speedup, while improving success rate in 14 pairs by up to 15.08 percentage points. These results show that action tails are not merely discarded predictions, but reusable intermediate states that enable more efficient and accurate VLA inference. These results demonstrate that the action tail can support the generation of better actions with fewer denoising steps. Our project is available at https://chenbao123ww.github.io/VLA-Rolling/ Sicheng He chen123ww@outlook.com; Email: zhangwenqi@zju.edu.cn; Xuhong Zhang zh"

Text:"SWIFT: Self-guided Weak-to-strong dIFfusion models via Target extrapolation Yanjin He1,2,, Cuifeng Shen2, Wanzhou Lei2,3,, Maohua Li2,4, Tao Lan2, Kan Liu2, Hanlin Tang,2, Qing Qu1 1University of Michigan, Ann Arbor 3University of California, Berkeley 2Alibaba Group 4Nanjing University Abstract Training high-quality diffusion models is computationally expensive, and inference-time guidance often adds further sampling cost. In this work, we introduce Swift, simple yet effective self-guided training method that uses frozen weak denoiser to improve stronger model without adding inference overhead. For each noisy input, Swift extrapolates the clean regression target away from the weak denoisers prediction, amplifying components that remain underfit. The weak guide can be an early training checkpoint, requires no backpropagation during main-model training, and is discarded afterward. The resulting model retains the original sampler and supports both conditional and unconditional generation without label dropout or an unconditional branch. We explain this mechanism through spectral analysis of finite-time population gradient flow in an RKHS. With an early-stopped weak guide, we prove that nonempty range of extrapolation strengths yields strictly lower posterior-mean error than matched standard training, despite shifting the regression target away from the posterior mean. Experiments on CIFAR-10 and ImageNet-256 demonstrate improvements across model scales and representations. In one ImageNet-256 configuration, Swift surpasses the baselines best FID using roughly half the total training compute, including guide preparation and training overhead. On RAEv2, it achieves FID 1.15 without inference-time guidance and 0.99 with guidance. These results establish target extrapolation as an effective approach to weak-to-strong diffusion training with efficient single-model inference. E-mail: yanjinhe@umich.edu, wanghaisheng.whs@alibaba-inc.com Diffusion models have achieved strong g"

Abstract Modelharness co-evolution has emerged as prominent paradigm for recursive self-improving (RSI) agents, jointly evolving their execution scaffolds and intrinsic capabilities. We study the persistent yet often overlooked brainbody mismatch problem in this paradigm, where an evolving harness (body) demands behaviors its model (brain) has not learned or restricts capabilities it already possesses. Specifically, we propose Neural Skinning Fields Autoencoder that predicts shape-dependent deformation subspaces without per-object network training. To learn these subspaces without simulation trajectories, we develop Physics-Informed Self-Supervised Learning strategy that combines skinning-field normalization with conflict-aware gradient correction, stabilizing joint optimization under physical and orthogonality constraints. The learned representation supports efficient reduced dynamics with user control and contact interactions, together with direct animation binding to meshes, point clouds, and 3D Gaussians. For prescribed rig animation, we further introduce self-supervised complementarity objective to learn complementary elastic responses without enforcing complementarity constraints at runtime. Experiments demonstrate cross-shape generalization of the learned skinning fields, real-time reduced simulation, and applications to controllable, collision-aware, and rig-complementary animation. However, item accuracy alone cannot establish whether models make correct decisions across controlled variations or assign reliable confidence to their predictions. We introduce DecisionPivot, diagnostic benchmark comprising 48 tasks and 40,033 items across seven decision roles. Its core track contains 7,800 items from 13 generated task families, organized into contrast groups with computed answers verified by independently implemented solvers. The complementary reference track contains 32,233 items from 35 public test sets, preserving their original evaluation metrics. We evaluate 40 decision models and 14 general models using documented, model-specific protocols, assessing item and contrast-group accuracy, probability ordering, calibration, and empirical selective coverage. Our results reveal substantial differences obscured by aggregate scores: two 27B models separated by only 2.6 points on the reference track achieve contrast-group accuracies of 70.1% and 44.2%. Across 2,400 binary contrast pairs, Perplexity Decider correctly ranks the positive member above the negative member in 92.3% of pairs but answers both members correctly in only 74.1%, exposing gap between probability ordering and decision correctness. Models with similar item accuracy also differ in the reliability of their high-confidence predictions. On separate 1,296-item subset, GLM-5.3 achieves 93.5% contrast-group accuracy at higher reasoning setting, compared with 81.5% at its lowest effort. By linking errors to pivotal details and difficulty levels, DecisionPivot provides fine-grained diagnostics of decision failures that aggregate evaluation alone cannot reveal. Data: Hugging Face Code: GitHub 1.LLM systems make decisions throughout the processing of request: which model should handle it, whether tool call is permitted, whether generated output is correct, and whether an agent has achieved its goal. decision model takes state, typed question, and finite set of options as input and assigns probability to each option. Many such models produce these predictions in single forward pass, enabling systems to act directly or defer uncertain cases for further reasoning or review. Reliable evaluation must therefore assess not only decision correctness but also sensitivity to decisive evidence and the reliability of predicted confidence. Existing evaluations adapt public test sets into decision tasks and assess calibration and selective prediction [2]. While these evaluations provide valuable comparisons, aggregate item accuracy does not reveal whether model correctly handles all members of controlled contrast group. Moreover, superficial cues can inflate performance: content-blind rule achieves 73.0% accuracy on llmbar, compared with 50% chance baseline (Table C2). Potential training overlap further complicates interpretation, as public documentation may identify training data sources without specifying which evaluation items were included. These limitations motivate combining established public benchmarks with controlled, independently verifiable decision cases. We introduce DecisionPivot, diagnostic benchmark spanning seven decision roles and comprising two complementary tracks. The core track contains 7,800 items from 13 generated task families, organized into contrast groups. In most families, modifying pivotal detail changes the correct decision while preserving the surrounding context, enabling controlled evaluation across related cases. Independently implemented solvers verify every core answer. DecisionPivot evaluates three complementary aspects of decision quality: whether individual predictions are correct, whether all members of contrast group are answered correctly, and whether predicted confidence reliably reflects correctness. We measure group-level correctness using pair accuracy, which requires every member of contrast group to be correct, including groups containing more than two items. We further assess probability ordering, calibration, and selective coverage to distinguish correct relative rankings from correct decisions and evaluate confidence reliability. We evaluate 40 decision models and 11 open general models on both tracks, plus three API chat models on the core track, using documented, model-specific protocols. Our results reveal substantial differences that aggregate scores alone do not capture (Figure 1). Text:"Harness Self-Evolution for Long-Horizon Terminal Agents Ruhan Wang1,2,, Yucheng Shi1,, Zongxia Li1,3, Junyao Yang1,5, Zhongzhi Li1,4, Yue Yu2, Haitao Mi1, Dongruo Zhou2, Leoweiliang1 1Tencent HY LLM Frontier 4University of Georgia This work was conducted during an internship at Tencent. 5National University of Singapore Internship mentor. 2Indiana University 3University of Maryland, College Park Long-horizon terminal agents may complete task in one run yet fail to do so consistently across repeated runs. Stochastic generation can introduce local errors that propagate through subsequent actions and context, creating gap between task-solving ability and reliable execution. The harness controls command execution, feedback, context management, and completion decisions, providing direct way to address this gap. The eﬀectiveness of these mechanisms depends on the models behavior. We present RiseHarness, framework for iteratively improving agent harnesses while keeping model weights ﬁxed. RiseHarness focuses on ﬂipping tasks, which have both successful and failed runs under the same conﬁguration. Successful runs establish that the current system can complete the task and provide references for analyzing failures. The analysis combines agent trajectories with internal harness traces collected by runtime monitor, examining both the models actions and how the harness executes them, presents feedback, and controls the interaction. Harness Handbook connects this evidence to speciﬁc harness behaviors and their source code, helping identify recurring problems in mechanisms shared across tasks. To address these problems, RiseHarness searches other harness frameworks for relevant implementations and uses them as design references, taking into account the current execution ﬂow, interaction protocol, available signals, and model behavior. Candidate changes undergo behavioral checks and repeated task evaluation; versions with measured gains become the basis for the next iteration. Thi"

Text:"More Memory, Worse Agents: Error Reproduction and Anti-Persistence in LLM Agents Ruhan Wang1, Kishan Panaganti2, Dongruo Zhou1 1Indiana University 2Tencent HY ruhwang@iu.edu, kpb@global.tencent.com, dz13@iu.edu LLM agents can improve across tasks without updating model parameters by storing experience from their own executions in external memory and reusing it to guide later decisions. central design question is whether to retain only observed trajectories and outcomes or also the rules, summaries, and skills inferred from them. Retaining these abstractions allows agents to reuse strategies without repeatedly deriving them from raw trajectories. However, errors in stored abstractions can propagate to subsequent decisions through retrieval and reuse. They can also shape execution trajectories, which then become the basis for new abstractions that reproduce the original errors. We term this feedback mechanism error reproduction: errors are regenerated through cycle of abstraction, execution, and re-abstraction, extending their persistence beyond direct reuse of the original faulty knowledge. We propose Anti-Persistence, memory design that accumulates trajectories and outcomes while reconstructing task-specific guidance on demand. An adaptive selector uses prior outcomes to decide whether and how to synthesize guidance, which is discarded after execution. This prevents its direct reuse as stored object, although indirect feedback through retained trajectories remains possible. Across different benchmarks and backbones, Anti-Persistence achieves higher overall held-out success and the lowest average LLM-call cost among the evaluated memory designs. These results support separating experience accumulation from abstraction persistence in memory-based self-improvement. Date: October 19, Large language model (LLM) agents solve tasks through multi-step interactions with an environment, producing trajectories of actions and observations [1]. We study memory-based self-improve"

Text:"TRACE: Tracking Multimodal Clinical Trajectories for Inpatient Risk Prediction Minjun Kim Jong Hak Moon*Accurate inpatient risk prediction requires modeling heterogeneous clinical evidence that accumulates irregularly over time. This challenge spans in-hospital mortality, prolonged length of stay, and readmission, each with task-specific observation windows. Existing multimodal approaches combine structured health record data, chest x-rays, and clinical text, but primarily focus on modality fusion, admission-level relational structure, or missing-modality compensation, with limited attention to how clinical evidence evolves as temporal trajectory over the course of an admission. We propose TRACE (TRAjectory modeling of multimodal Clinical Events), daily-scale globallocal framework that organizes recorded clinical events into day-level trajectory, separating day-invariant global context, such as demographics and diagnosis-derived information, from day-specific local events drawn from structured EHR data, chest x-rays, radiology reports, and discharge summaries. The resulting trajectory is encoded using temporal globallocal attention, allowing unavailable modalities to emit no tokens rather than being imputed. phenotype-guided contrastive objective further organizes admissions according to both outcome status and diagnosis-derived phenotype, providing additional structure under heterogeneous disease distributions. We construct cohorts from MIMICIV, eICU, and MIMIC-III comprising nearly 180,000 admissions and ICU stays, and evaluate TRACE on in-hospital mortality, prolonged length of stay, and 30-day readmission prediction. TRACE achieves the highest AUROC across all nine datasettask settings and the highest ACC in eight, compared with fusioncentered, relational, and missing-modality-aware baselines. Ablation studies show that the temporal global local attention mask achieves the highest AUROC across all three tasks on MIMIC-IV, while phenotypeguided contrastive learning improves mean phenotypestratified AUROC across all nine datasettask settings. Trajectory-level attribution further localizes the hospital days and modalities associated with changes in predicted risk. Keywords: Inpatient risk prediction, multimodal clinical trajectory modeling, electronic health records, temporal attention, supervised contrastive learning Equal contribution. Corresponding author: Jong Hak Moon. Both authors are with Yeji (mj.kim@yejix.ai; jh.moon@yejix.ai). Inpatient risk prediction is challenging because patient status evolves over time and is documented through heterogeneous, irregularly recorded clinical events [111]. During hospitalization, clinicians integrate relatively stable patient context, such as demographics and diagnosis-derived information, with evolving signals, including laboratory measurements, medications, procedures, imaging findings, and clinical notes [610]. These data do not form complete or uniformly sampled multimodal record. Instead, each admission contains patientand dataset-specific subset of modalities, and the meaning of the recorded events depends not only on what is observed, but also on when it occurs and how it changes over time [68]. Inpatient risk prediction is commonly formulated as admission-level binary classification, in which the model estimates the probability of an adverse outcome, such as prolonged length of stay (Long LOS), inhospital mortality, and 30-day readmission [1, 2, 611]. The clinically appropriate observation window differs across these binary prediction tasks. Long LOS prediction is most useful early in the admission for resource planning [1214], whereas in-hospital mortality prediction and 30-day readmission prediction may use data recorded over the completed hospitalization [2, 610]. unified trajectory model should therefore support task-specific observation windows while preserving the temporal organization of the clinical record. The central modeling challenge lies in how the admission representation is constructed within each observation window. Compressing the available data into single static representation can obscure day-specific changes in patient status [4, 5], while conventional multimodal fusion often assumes fixed input structure that does not reflect the incomplete and irregular nature of real-world clinical records [69, 11, 15, 16]. rigid organization of inputs may also fail to distinguish early prediction settings from completed-admission assessment. Prior clinical AI models have addressed important aspects of this problem. Multimodal fusion methods combine structured EHR, imaging, and clinical text [2, 69, 15]; graph-based approaches [10] incorporate admission similarity or relational structure; and missing-modality methods [68] improve learning when some modalities are unavailable. These directions have substantially advanced admission-level clinical prediction, but with limited attention to how clinical evidence evolves as temporal trajectory over the course of an 1 Figure 1: Overview of TRACE. Admission-level global categories and local inputs recorded within the task-specific observation window are encoded using frozen pretrained vision or language encoders, projected into shared space, and augmented with modality and day embeddings. For visual brevity, the category-specific global tokens Gi are shown collectively as Gcls. Global tokens, day-specific local tokens L(k) , and learnable [TASK] and [PHENO] tokens are encoded by masked Transformer using the temporal globallocal attention mask ATGL, which keeps global context accessible, preserves temporal order among local events, and allows [PHENO] to attend to [TASK] but not the reverse. The encoded [TASK] and [PHENO] states produce ˆp(k) and r(k), respectively, and are jointly optimized using L(k) = L(k) pheno. Snowflake and flame icons indicate frozen and trainable modules. pred + λL(k) admission. Less directly addressed is how to construct unified admission representation that reflects this trajectory at the day level, separates stable global context from evolving local events, and accommodates task-specific temporal windows across datasets with different modality availability. We propose TRACE (TRAjectory modeling of multimodal Clinical Events), daily-scale globallocal framework for multimodal inpatient risk prediction. TRACE retains the admission-level prediction objective but changes how the clinical record is represented before the final task-specific prediction. For "

Text:"Saaket Agashe*, Yuzhe Yang*, Gonzalo Gonzalez-Pumariega, Bhuvan Koduru, Chih-Lun Li, Jiachen Yang, Ang Li, Xin Eric Wang Computer-use agents are limited by the interfaces humans give them. An agent may type spreadsheet cell by cell when single line of code would do, or print all two thousand lines of file through shell to find one function. Both the interfaces and the knowledge of when to use each are built by hand, which cannot keep pace with new applications, workflows, and models. We argue that interface design should itself be an agent capability. We introduce Agent S4, an autonomous computer-use scientist that builds Evolving Agent Interfaces from task experience. meta-agent examines task agents trajectories on synthetic training tasks, without success labels, and constructs and revises interfaces and usage knowledge in repository. Population-based evolution then selects which repositories to develop further by their performance on validation tasks the meta-agent never sees, favoring changes that generalize beyond the seen tasks. On OSWorld V2, Agent S4, with Qwen3.8-27B and the evolved repository of interfaces, sets new state of the art in open-source computer-use agents, reaching 21.3% binary score, 3.8 times the same model with fixed interfaces. On ScienceBoard, the evolved interfaces improve the success rate from 41.4% to 58.6% with the same model. Abstract FocusGUI is a lightweight adapter that dynamically selects relevant multimodal evidence for GUI agents to prevent compounding errors from irrelevant context, improving action accuracy without fine-tuning the backbone.  Generated by thinkingmachines/Inkling-Small Graphical User Interface (GUI) agents increasingly act over streaming omni-modal observations spanning screenshots, audio, video, interaction history, and device states. However, exposing this complete context uniformly at every step ignores that relevance is transient and sparse: the signal determining one action can become clutter at the next. Because executed actions feed back into the context, these errors compound into Sequential Distraction, driving the agent into irreversible trajectory drift. We formulate the underlying requirement as Focused Perception: a representation that is step-adaptive, action-sufficient, and non-intrusive. We realize this in FocusGUI, a lightweight adapter that dynamically prioritizes multimodal channels against a step-specific decision reference. Instead of uniformly processing all inputs, it compresses the most relevant evidence into a compact latent and subtly injects it into the frozen decoder's embeddings, guiding the model's attention without altering its native parameter space. FocusGUI trains from action supervision alone, requiring neither modality-relevance labels nor backbone fine-tuning. Across four omni-modal backbones, it raises overall Type Match by +33.2 and Exact Match by 3.0 times over the frozen backbone on average, and Type Match by +30.1 over the strongest existing enhancement method.
[21.10.2026 07:03] Response: {"desc": "В статье рассматриваются GUI-агенты, которые действуют по потоковым мультимодальным данным (скриншоты, аудио, видео, история взаимодействий), и показано, что нерелевантный контекст приводит к каскадным ошибкам, названным «Sequential Distraction». Авторы предлагают принцип Focused Perception и реализуют его в FocusGUI — легковесном адаптере, который динамически выбирает наиболее значимые модальности, сжимает их в компактный латентный вектор и внедряет в эмбеддинги замороженного декодера. Метод обучается только на данных о действиях, без разметки релевантности модальностей и без дообучения backbone. В экспериментах на четырёх omni-modal моделях FocusGUI в среднем повышает Type Match на 33.2 пункта и Exact Match в 3 раза относительно замороженной модели.", "emoji": "🎯", "title": "Фокус на главном: адаптер, который не даёт GUI-агенту сбиться с пути"}

Generated by thinkingmachines/Inkling-Small Graphical User Interface (GUI) agents increasingly act over streaming omni-modal observations spanning screenshots, audio, video, interaction history, and device states. Abstract Sage-A1.5 is a 35B mixture-of-experts agentic model that improves long-horizon problem solving via adaptive task delegation, verification, and open scientific exploration.  Generated by thinkingmachines/Inkling-Small In this report, we introduce Sage-A1.5, a 35B Mixture-of-Experts agentic model that builds on A1 to advance long-horizon problem solving through adaptive capability composition. Compared with A1, Sage-A1.5 learns not only to solve individual tasks, but also to organize work and revise its approach during execution. Sage-A1.5 highlights three improvements: agentic orchestration, verification, and open scientific discovery. First, agentic orchestration extends single-agent execution through task delegation and parallel collaboration among subagents with isolated contexts, enabling complex tasks to be decomposed and addressed concurrently and supporting efficient execution while reducing interference across concurrent subagents. Second, agentic verification strengthens long-horizon reliability through progress checking, evidence validation, and reflective strategy revision, allowing the agent to recognize unproductive search paths and pursue alternative approaches. Third, open scientific discovery extends agentic capabilities beyond answering predefined questions to conducting exploratory research, connecting scientific inquiry with interactive analysis and inspectable research artifacts. We showcase these capabilities through complicated agentic tasks and multidisciplinary scientific applications. Besides, we hope Sage-A1.5 provides the community with an accessible foundation for open scientific discovery, offering a practical path from long-horizon task execution to collaborative, verifiable, and inspectable scientific exploration.

Sage-A1.5 highlights three improvements: agentic orchestration, verification, and open scientific discovery. Third, open scientific discovery extends agentic capabilities beyond answering predefined questions to conducting exploratory research, connecting scientific inquiry with interactive analysis and inspectable research artifacts. We showcase these capabilities through complicated agentic tasks and multidisciplinary scientific applications. Besides, we hope Sage-A1.5 provides the community with an accessible foundation for open scientific discovery, offering a practical path from long-horizon task execution to collaborative, verifiable, and inspectable scientific exploration."

Abstract Progress-Aligned Recurrence trains looped language models to match intermediate states and stabilize recurrent norms, enabling strong length extrapolation on sequential tracking tasks.  Generated by thinkingmachines/Inkling-Small Looped language models can run more iterations at test time, but additional iterations need not help them solve longer problems. For sequential state tracking, final-answer supervision does not specify how far each iteration should advance, and repeated updates can change the scale of the recurrent state. We introduce Progress-Aligned Recurrence (PAR), which trains the prediction at iteration k to match the state after the first k operations and rescales recurrent states to their first-iteration norms. On swap-S_5, a Qwen2.5-0.5B model trained on at most 32 moves achieves 95.5\% mean full-state accuracy at 512 moves, or 16times the training length, compared with 55.7\% reported for RLT and 0.8\% for explicit chain of thought on the same backbone. Controlled S_5 ablations show that removing norm anchoring reduces the extrapolation factor from 16times to below 2.2times, and replacing progress-aligned targets with final-answer supervision reduces it to at most 8times. A learned completion readout selects when to answer without a supplied stopping depth. When intermediate labels are unavailable, prefix self-distillation induces the correspondence from shorter problem prefixes using only final answers. The gains apply across sequential state-tracking tasks, establishing the task structure required for depth extrapolation. These findings support training intermediate predictions and state scale together to make additional recurrent depth useful for longer problems.
[21.10.2026 07:03] Response: {"desc": "Статья предлагает метод Progress-Aligned Recurrence (PAR) для looped language models, то есть рекуррентных LLM, которые могут выполнять больше итераций на этапе инференса. Идея в том, что на каждой итерации k предсказание обучается соответствовать состоянию после первых k операций, а норма рекуррентного состояния нормализуется к норме первой итерации. На задаче отслеживания состояний swap-S_5 модель, обученная максимум на 32 ходах, достигает 95.5% точности на 512 ходах, что примерно в 16 раз больше длины обучения. Абляции показывают, что и выравнивание по прогрессу, и нормализация масштаба состояния критичны для экстраполяции по глубине, а prefix self-distillation позволяет обойтись без промежуточных разметок.", "emoji": "🔁", "title": "Учим рекуррентную модель идти шаг за шагом и не терять масштаб"}

Abstract Video-Point Model predicts future multi-view videos and 3D point trajectories from video histories and instructions, improving forecasting and robot control through shared 3D-aware representations.  Generated by thinkingmachines/Inkling-Small Reliable multi-view forecasting requires videos from different cameras to describe the same evolving 3D scene. To this end, we introduce Video-Point Model (VPM), a world model that conditions on multi-view video histories and a language instruction to jointly forecast future videos from arbitrary viewpoints and the 3D trajectories of persistent scene points. We extend a pretrained world model with camera-conditioned multi-view processing that integrates complementary observations across cameras, and add a trajectory head that predicts how physical points move through time. Each trajectory tracks a single point across all views, and supervising these trajectories alongside video generation therefore anchors appearance changes in every camera to a shared description of 3D motion. To support training and evaluation, we build VPM-Data, a simulation corpus pairing synchronized multi-view videos with persistent point trajectories, and VP-WorldBench, a forecasting benchmark spanning simulated and real environments. We further extend VPM to robot control by adding robot actions as a token modality in the generator. Action tokens share the point-supervised backbone and are denoised jointly with future videos, thereby grounding the policy in the same 3D-aware representation. On VP-WorldBench and existing 4D generation benchmarks, point supervision improves both video fidelity and 3D motion accuracy over a video-only counterpart, and in simulated and real-robot experiments, it also raises the manipulation success of the action-enabled model.
[21.10.2026 07:03] Response: {"desc": "Авторы представляют Video-Point Model (VPM) — world model, которая по истории многокамерного видео и языковой инструкции одновременно предсказывает будущие видео с произвольных ракурсов и 3D-траектории постоянных точек сцены. Модель расширяет предобученный backbone механизмом camera-conditioned multi-view обработки и дополнительной trajectory head, а обучение на траекториях точек связывает изменения внешнего вида во всех камерах с единым описанием 3D-движения. Для обучения и оценки создан датасет VPM-Data из синхронизированных симуляционных видео и VP-WorldBench для прогнозирования в симулированных и реальных средах. Расширение моделью действий робота как токенов позволяет использовать ту же 3D-aware представление для управления роботом, что повышает точность движения и успешность манипуляций.", "emoji": "🤖", "title": "Видим сцену в 3D — предсказываем её будущее и управляем роботом"}

Abstract SolarRenderer adapts a frozen video backbone with lightweight LoRAs to render realistic videos from diverse structural inputs in four steps or real-time streaming, using frame-aligned training data.  Generated by thinkingmachines/Inkling-Small Game engines and coding agents can specify the layout, motion, and camera of a scene, yet turning such coarse structure into realistic video requires a renderer that follows the structure and completes the appearance it leaves open. Existing renderers are tied to the representation of one engine, need tens of denoising steps or a costly distillation of their own, and learn from conditions that are not aligned with their target videos. We present SolarRenderer, a structure-conditioned video renderer that adapts a frozen MiniMax-H3 33B backbone with two lightweight LoRAs. Structural videos enter the native reference pathway of the backbone at the positions of the frames they describe, without a type identifier and described only in plain text, so that a single control LoRA follows arbitrary structural representations. Starting from a pretrained four-step LoRA, structure-guided distribution matching renders in four steps, 12.3 times faster than 50-step sampling, while preserving the learned control. A block-causal variant extends the same design to streaming and renders 768p video in real time. To train the model, we build a frame-aligned corpus of 136K clips from game engines, simulators, and YouTube videos. Experiments on an in-distribution test set and a generalization benchmark of 16 structural representations show that SolarRenderer achieves state-of-the-art results across both, and generalizes to 11 unseen representations, including pose skeletons, drawings, and point clouds.

Она адаптирует замороженный видео-бэкбон MiniMax-H3 33B с помощью двух легковесных LoRA, подавая структурные видео напрямую в нативный reference-путь модели без указания типа входа. Благодаря structure-guided distribution matching рендеринг занимает четыре шага вместо пятидесяти и в 12.3 раза быстрее, а block-causal вариант позволяет стриминг в 768p в реальном времени. Prompt: Read an abstract of the ML paper and return a JSON with fields: 'desc': explanation of the paper in Russian (4 sentences), use correct machine learning terms. Abstract VLA-Rolling improves vision-language-action model efficiency by reusing discarded action tails with adaptive denoising budgets, reducing inference cost and boosting success rates.  Generated by thinkingmachines/Inkling-Small Flow-matching vision-language-action (VLA) models generate an action chunk by iteratively denoising Gaussian noise, but typically execute only a short prefix before replanning with updated observations. Under this receding-horizon paradigm, the unexecuted remainder---the action tail---is discarded, and the next chunk is regenerated from fresh noise. We show that this discarded tail retains transferable denoising progress: action-tail initialization can match or outperform a native 10-step Gaussian baseline using fewer denoising steps. However, the optimal denoising budget varies substantially across samples, indicating that tail reuse should be adapted to the updated environment. Based on this observation, we introduce VLA-Rolling, a lightweight inference framework that reuses action tails through three stages: action-tail inheritance, per-action confidence estimation, and confidence-guided refinement through renoising and denoising budget allocation, while the underlying VLA remains frozen. Across π_{0.5}, SmolVLA, X-VLA, and MolmoAct2 on LIBERO, MetaWorld, LIBERO-Plus, and RoboTwin 2.0, VLA-Rolling reduces action-chunk inference cost in all 16 model--benchmark pairs, achieving up to a 1.48times speedup, while improving success rate in 14 pairs by up to 15.08 percentage points. These results show that action tails are not merely discarded predictions, but reusable intermediate states that enable more efficient and accurate VLA inference. These results demonstrate that the action tail can support the generation of better actions with fewer denoising steps. Our project is available at https://chenbao123ww.github.io/VLA-Rolling/.
[21.10.2026 07:04] Response: {"desc": "Авторы предлагают VLA-Rolling — лёгкий фреймворк для ускорения flow-matching VLA-моделей, замороженных при инференсе. Вместо того чтобы выбрасывать невыполненную часть action chunk (action tail), метод переиспользует её как промежуточное состояние для инициализации следующего денойзинга. Бюджет шагов денойзинга адаптивно выбирается по per-action confidence, а уточнение выполняется через renoising. На LIBERO, MetaWorld, LIBERO-Plus и RoboTwin 2.0 метод снижает стоимость инференса до 1.48x и повышает success rate в большинстве случаев.", "emoji": "🤖", "title": "Не выбрасывай хвост действий: переиспользуй его для более быстрых и точных решений"}

Abstract Ropedia W0 is a world foundation model for cross-embodiment loco-manipulation that unifies human and robot data via kinematic representations and achieves state-of-the-art results across diverse benchmarks.  Generated by thinkingmachines/Inkling-Small World models enable embodied agents to anticipate action consequences, yet jointly modeling locomotion and manipulation across diverse embodiments remains challenging due to heterogeneous action spaces and limited multimodal supervision. We present Ropedia W0, the first egocentric loco-manipulation world foundation model spanning humans and robots with diverse embodiments. We curate Xp-Data, comprising 10k hours of egocentric human data and 3k hours of robot data with unified multimodal annotations and systematic quality control. A unified kinematic representation encodes locomotion through camera poses and manipulation through projected skeletons, with robot skeletons combining embodiment-specific forward kinematics with extensive manual keypoint annotation and correction. We also introduce Xp-Teleop, a long-horizon loco-manipulation benchmark based on high-quality dexterous humanoid teleoperation data. Evaluations demonstrate state-of-the-art performance across five cross-embodiment benchmarks (DreamDojo, Ψ_0, ω-HOME, Xp-Teleop, and AgiBot) and on held-out human egocentric video, from zero-shot inference to few-shot and full-data post-training. Scaling human pre-training data improves performance on both human and robot evaluations. After distillation, our model supports real-time, minute-long rollouts, enabling live VR interaction. Applications in policy evaluation, model-based planning, and world-action modeling further demonstrate the model's utility for embodied decision-making.

Prompt: Read an abstract of the ML paper and return a JSON with fields: 'desc': explanation of the paper in Russian (4 sentences), use correct machine learning terms. Abstract A correction module trained to refine diffusion predictions reduces exposure bias, improving unguided sample quality and enabling distillation to eliminate inference overhead.  Generated by thinkingmachines/Inkling-Small Iterative sampling in diffusion models can lead to error accumulation due to exposure bias. The denoiser is trained on real images interpolated with noise, but at inference its inputs are constructed from its own imperfect predictions from the earlier steps, something it has never seen at training time. This might be one reason diffusion models often struggle without additional help, such as CFG. We introduce a correction step for diffusion models which attempts to reduce this training-inference gap. The corrector takes the base model's prediction as input and is trained against the same ground-truth target. This makes correction an explicit learning task, with training inputs provided by the base model. During sampling, at each denoising step, we iteratively refine the denoiser's clean-sample prediction. This allows us to correct predictions at intermediate denoising steps, preventing early artifacts from persisting into the final sample. Although the corrector is trained only on predictions from the base denoiser, it can be applied repeatedly to its own outputs at inference for better results. This simple framework leads to substantial improvement in sample quality while largely preserving diversity. We evaluate our method across DiT, SiT, and JiT. Our method substantially improves upon previously reported FID scores for unguided generation and, notably, unconditional generation, where producing high-quality samples without guidance has been particularly challenging. Our method achieves performance competitive with classifier-free guidance (CFG), and combining correction with CFG further improves diversity and reduces oversaturation typical of CFG. Finally, we show that the additional inference cost of correction can be amortized by distilling the refined predictions back into the base diffusion model, retaining the quality gains without additional inference-time overhead.

Корректор обучается на предсказаниях базовой модели с тем же ground-truth таргетом и итеративно уточняет чистое предсказание на каждом шаге денойзинга. Метод заметно улучшает качество генерации без guidance, включая безусловную генерацию, и даёт результаты, сопоставимые с CFG, при этом сохраняя разнообразие. Abstract Swift improves diffusion model training by extrapolating regression targets away from a frozen weak denoiser, reducing error and training cost without inference overhead.  Generated by thinkingmachines/Inkling-Small Training high-quality diffusion models is computationally expensive, and inference-time guidance often adds further sampling cost. In this work, we introduce Swift, a simple yet effective self-guided training method that uses a frozen weak denoiser to improve a stronger model without adding inference overhead. For each noisy input, Swift extrapolates the clean regression target away from the weak denoiser's prediction, amplifying components that remain underfit. The weak guide can be an early training checkpoint, requires no backpropagation during main-model training, and is discarded afterward. The resulting model retains the original sampler and supports both conditional and unconditional generation without label dropout or an unconditional branch. We explain this mechanism through a spectral analysis of finite-time population gradient flow in an RKHS. With an early-stopped weak guide, we prove that a nonempty range of extrapolation strengths yields strictly lower posterior-mean error than matched standard training, despite shifting the regression target away from the posterior mean. Experiments on CIFAR-10 and ImageNet-256 demonstrate improvements across model scales and representations. In one ImageNet-256 configuration, Swift surpasses the baseline's best FID using roughly half the total training compute, including guide preparation and training overhead. On RAEv2, it achieves FID 1.15 without inference-time guidance and 0.99 with guidance. These results establish target extrapolation as an effective approach to weak-to-strong diffusion training with efficient single-model inference.

Abstract CoEvo-RSI reduces brain-body mismatch in recursive self-improving agents by using bidirectional historical inheritance to align model capabilities with evolving harness requirements, achieving strong results via supervised fine-tuning.  Generated by thinkingmachines/Inkling-Small Model--harness co-evolution has emerged as a prominent paradigm for recursive self-improving (RSI) agents, jointly evolving their execution scaffolds and intrinsic capabilities. We study the persistent yet often overlooked brain--body mismatch problem in this paradigm, where an evolving harness (body) demands behaviors its model (brain) has not learned or restricts capabilities it already possesses. We introduce CoEvo-RSI, a RSI framework that advances model--harness co-evolution through agent-driven, bidirectional historical inheritance. Drawing on a shared evolutionary archive and execution feedback, a meta-agent coordinates harness development from historical designs with model checkpoint selection or merging and subsequent adaptation under the selected harness, aligning inherited capabilities with evolving execution requirements to reduce brain--body mismatch. Using only supervised fine-tuning (SFT) on its own rejection-sampled trajectories, CoEvo-RSI makes Qwen3.5-9B competitive with larger models across diverse vertical applications, including multi-agent failure attribution, role-aware text-to-SQL, and smart-home instruction following. It surpasses DeepSeek-V4-Pro on TraceElephant (+14.16 agent accuracy) and GLM-5.3 on HomeBench VM (+4.90 success rate), underscoring the need for brain--body alignment and the potential of bidirectional historical inheritance to advance agent co-evolution.

Авторы вводят двунаправленное наследование истории из общего эволюционного архива, а meta-agent выбирает архитектуру harness и checkpoint модели или их merging с последующей адаптацией. Abstract MMEnterprise-Bench evaluates multimodal agents on reasoning over evolving workplace records with conflicts and missing evidence.  Generated by thinkingmachines/Inkling-Small Enterprise agents answer questions over workplace records that evolve through team collaboration. As information is shared, corrected, and revised across documents, chats, and figures, multiple versions of the same information accumulate in the workspace. Reliable question answering therefore requires agents to reason over sufficient evidence, resolve cross-source and temporal conflicts, and recognize when the available evidence is insufficient to establish an answer. To systematically evaluate these capabilities, we introduce MMEnterprise-Bench, a benchmark comprising 1,613 questions grounded in evolving multimodal records from simulated collaboration projects, covering three categories: Evidence Reasoning, Conflict Resolution, and Unanswerability. We evaluate 10 proprietary and open-source multimodal LLMs and find that even the strongest models struggle when evidence contains cross-source or temporal conflicts or spans multiple images. Moreover, increasing the retrieval budget or employing a larger retriever improves evidence recall but does not consistently translate into higher answer accuracy. These findings highlight the persistent challenges of reasoning over evolving workplace records and establish MMEnterprise-Bench as a testbed for developing more reliable enterprise agents. Code and data are available at https://mlvlab.github.io/MMEnterprise-Bench/.
[21.10.2026 07:04] Response: {"desc": "Авторы представляют MMEnterprise-Bench — бенчмарк для оценки мультимодальных AI-агентов, которые отвечают на вопросы по корпоративным рабочим записям, постоянно меняющимся в ходе командной работы. Набор содержит 1613 вопросов по трём категориям: Evidence Reasoning, Conflict Resolution и Unanswerability, где агент должен распознать недостаточность данных. Протестированы 10 мультимодальных LLM, и даже лучшие модели плохо справляются с конфликтами между источниками, временными противоречиями и несколькими изображениями. Показано, что увеличение бюджета retrieval или более крупный retriever повышает полноту найденных доказательств, но не всегда улучшает точность ответов.", "emoji": "🏢", "title": "Корпоративные AI-агенты пока не умеют разбираться в противоречивых данных"}

To systematically evaluate these capabilities, we introduce MMEnterprise-Bench, a benchmark comprising 1,613 questions grounded in evolving multimodal records from simulated collaboration projects, covering three categories: Evidence Reasoning, Conflict Resolution, and Unanswerability. We evaluate 10 proprietary and open-source multimodal LLMs and find that even the strongest models struggle when evidence contains cross-source or temporal conflicts or spans multiple images. Moreover, increasing the retrieval budget or employing a larger retriever improves evidence recall but does not consistently translate into higher answer accuracy. These findings highlight the persistent challenges of reasoning over evolving workplace records and establish MMEnterprise-Bench as a testbed for developing more reliable enterprise agents. Code and data are available at https://mlvlab.github.io/MMEnterprise-Bench/."

Abstract PhysSkin++ learns continuous neural skinning fields from static shapes to enable real-time, physics-based animation across diverse geometries and spatial representations without per-object training.  Generated by thinkingmachines/Inkling-Small Generalizing real-time physics-based animation across diverse 3D shapes and spatial discretizations remains challenging. In this paper, we introduce PhysSkin++, a physics-informed framework that learns continuous neural skinning fields from static geometries to construct compact deformation subspaces. Specifically, we propose a Neural Skinning Fields Autoencoder that predicts shape-dependent deformation subspaces without per-object network training. To learn these subspaces without simulation trajectories, we develop a Physics-Informed Self-Supervised Learning strategy that combines skinning-field normalization with conflict-aware gradient correction, stabilizing joint optimization under physical and orthogonality constraints. The learned representation supports efficient reduced dynamics with user control and contact interactions, together with direct animation binding to meshes, point clouds, and 3D Gaussians. For prescribed rig animation, we further introduce a self-supervised complementarity objective to learn complementary elastic responses without enforcing complementarity constraints at runtime. Experiments demonstrate cross-shape generalization of the learned skinning fields, real-time reduced simulation, and applications to controllable, collision-aware, and rig-complementary animation.

Autoencoder предсказывает компактные подпространства деформаций, зависящие от формы, без дообучения сети под каждый объект. Self-supervised обучение с физическими ограничениями, нормализацией полей и conflict-aware коррекцией градиентов стабилизирует оптимизацию. Это позволяет проводить real-time reduced-симуляцию с управлением, учётом контактов и привязкой анимации к мешам, облакам точек и 3D Gaussians.", "emoji": "🦴", "title": "Физическая анимация любых 3D-форм в реальном времени без переобучения"}

Abstract S-Agent 2.0 improves spatial reasoning by integrating a persistent scene memory and plain-language interpretation into a coding agent with 3D perception tools.  Generated by thinkingmachines/Inkling-Small Spatial reasoning asks a model to work out where objects are in 3D space, how they relate to one another, and how they move. Agentic VLMs have recently brought large improvements on such tasks by letting the model actively gather evidence with 3D perception tools instead of answering in a single pass. Even so, on many questions the tools return the right evidence and the model still does not arrive at the correct answer: the gap between model and tools, where raw readings become usable conclusions, is left unfilled. We present S-Agent 2.0, a spatial reasoning agent that equips an off-the-shelf general coding agent with a command-line perception toolkit, and we design the interface around one principle: help the VLM close the evidence loop: acquire scene evidence, remember it in a persistent scene memory, and interpret each reading in plain spatial language. This design keeps the full flexibility of a coding agent, and every reading it acts on stays grounded in the tools' geometry. On MMSI-Bench and ReVSI, the toolkit improves a strong open-source model, Qwen3.8-27B, by +13.9 and +20.5 points over the same agent without tools, and a strong closed-source model, Gemini-3.8-Flash, by +11.2 and +9.6 points. With the open backbone S-Agent 2.0 reaches 66.5 on MMSI-Bench and 82.3 on ReVSI, ahead of the specialized system SpatialClaw on the same backbone (62.6 and 74.5), and with Gemini-3.8-Flash it reaches 74.1 and 90.5 against its 67.0 and 79.7. Both tool gains shrink as the backbone grows more capable, but SpatialClaw's shrinks far more on ReVSI, from +13.2 to +2.7, while ours stays at +9.6. S-Agent 2.0 also works across different general coding agent harnesses, such as Codex and Claude Code.
[21.10.2026 07:04] Response: {"desc": "Статья представляет S-Agent 2.0 — агента для пространственного рассуждения, построенного на базе универсального coding agent с набором инструментов 3D-восприятия через командную строку. Ключевая идея: помочь VLM замкнуть цикл доказательств, то есть собирать данные о сцене, хранить их в персистентной памяти сцены и интерпретировать показания на простом пространственном языке. На бенчмарках MMSI-Bench и ReVSI инструменты заметно повышают качество моделей Qwen3.8-27B и Gemini-3.8-Flash, а открытая версия превосходит специализированную систему SpatialClaw. Подход также работает с разными coding agent harness, такими как Codex и Claude Code.", "emoji": "🧭", "title": "От сырых измерений к пространственному пониманию: замыкаем цикл доказательств"}

Abstract DRLMs refine draft answers via parallel token revisions and a single-pass cavity energy evaluation, outperforming autoregressive and diffusion baselines on reasoning tasks.  Generated by thinkingmachines/Inkling-Small Drafting an answer allows a reasoning model to reconsider each decision in the context of the others. Yet repeated diffusion denoising does not ensure that individually plausible token updates form a better answer together. We introduce Draft Refinement Language Models (DRLMs), which use a single denoiser to propose parallel revisions and evaluate a candidate draft. We train the denoiser to reconstruct every token from uniformly corrupted drafts, teaching both preservation and repair. Its predictions specify revisions directly, without a diffusion reverse transition. Evaluation requires a different use of these predictions: confidence in an existing token reflects both contextual support and evidence from observing the token itself. Using the known corruption process, we correct for this self-token evidence to estimate how well each token is supported by the prompt and the remaining draft. Aggregating these estimates yields a cavity energy computed in one forward pass, without an additional scoring model. Each round proposes a revision, reads the completed candidate in its revised context, and retains it only if its energy is lower. Across Countdown, Sudoku-Extreme, and Maze-Hard, our method outperforms autoregressive and discrete diffusion baselines. With 6M parameters, DRLM reaches 44.4% on Countdown-5 and 93.9% on Sudoku-Extreme. The results establish complete-draft parallel refinement as an effective alternative to reverse diffusion for complex reasoning.

Модель обучается восстанавливать токены из равномерно искажённых черновиков, без обратного перехода диффузии. Для оценки качества кандидата используется cavity energy, вычисляемая за один проход вперёд с поправкой на собственный вклад токена. Метод превосходит авторегрессионные и дискретные диффузионные модели на задачах Countdown, Sudoku-Extreme и Maze-Hard при всего 6M параметрах.", "emoji": "🧩", "title": "Переписывать весь черновик целиком вместо пошагового шумоподавления"}

Abstract StreamingSwap is a real-time flow-matching framework for video face swapping that improves identity fidelity and efficiency via joint identity-reconstruction loss and reduced spatial-temporal generation, achieving stable 20 FPS performance on long videos.  Generated by thinkingmachines/Inkling-Small Video face swapping transfers the reference identity to a target video while preserving its other attributes and motion. Despite recent progress in reference-guided flow-based methods, jointly achieving faithful identity transfer, high fidelity, and real-time generation over long video streams remains challenging. To address this challenge, we rethink the task-specific structure of reference-guided video face swapping. First, face swapping changes only identity while preserving most visual content, yet flow-matching supervision is weakly sensitive to these changes. We therefore develop Joint Rec-ID Loss, which balances explicit identity supervision and latent reconstruction according to the noise level. Second, the target video already provides non-edited attributes and temporal dynamics, enabling generation with fewer spatial tokens and shorter temporal contexts through Face-RoI Generation and Short-Context Rollout Training. By integrating these designs, we propose StreamingSwap, a reference-guided flow-matching framework for real-time streaming video face swapping. We also introduce HumanFaceVid, comprising 1,000 hours of cinematic training data and evaluation sets of 200 short and 200 long videos. Experiments demonstrate that our method achieves competitive identity transfer and visual quality at 20 FPS on a single NVIDIA RTX 4090 GPU, with stable generation over videos up to 10 minutes long. Project webpage: https://aim-uofa.github.io/StreamingSwap/
[21.10.2026 07:05] Response: {"desc": "Статья представляет StreamingSwap — фреймворк на основе flow-matching для замены лиц в видео в реальном времени с сохранением идентичности. Ключевые идеи: Joint Rec-ID Loss, который балансирует explicit identity supervision и latent reconstruction в зависимости от уровня шума, и генерация только по области лица (Face-RoI Generation) с короткими временными контекстами (Short-Context Rollout Training). Авторы также представляют датасет HumanFaceVid с 1000 часами кинематографических данных и тестовыми наборами видео. Abstract Specter accelerates single-GPU mixture-of-experts inference by combining quantized speculative decoding with expert offloading to hide PCIe latency and improve throughput.  Generated by thinkingmachines/Inkling-Small Mixture-of-experts (MoE) models often exceed a single accelerator's memory capacity. Offloading experts to host memory creates a severe PCIe I/O bottleneck because data-dependent routing keeps transfers on the critical path. We present Specter, an inference system that uses quantization to combine speculative decoding with expert offloading. Its lightweight quantized draft model runs efficiently on device while preserving high-fidelity expert activation patterns. This provides accurate multi-step lookahead over future expert usage, allowing the runtime to organize expert movement and execution ahead of verification and improve both communication and execution efficiency. Under single-request inference, Specter achieves an average 1.50x (up to 1.88x) speedup in time per output token (TPOT) over the state-of-the-art system. Specter makes memory-constrained, single-GPU MoE inference more practical on commodity hardware. Our code is available at https://github.com/wwwwwf/specter_asplos.
[21.10.2026 07:05] Response: {"desc": "Статья представляет Specter — систему для инференса Mixture-of-Experts (MoE) моделей на одном GPU, когда веса экспертов не помещаются в память видеокарты. Paper text to classify:

"Abstract Specter accelerates single-GPU mixture-of-experts inference by combining quantized speculative decoding with expert offloading to hide PCIe latency and improve throughput.  Generated by thinkingmachines/Inkling-Small Mixture-of-experts (MoE) models often exceed a single accelerator's memory capacity. Offloading experts to host memory creates a severe PCIe I/O bottleneck because data-dependent routing keeps transfers on the critical path. We present Specter, an inference system that uses quantization to combine speculative decoding with expert offloading. Its lightweight quantized draft model runs efficiently on device while preserving high-fidelity expert activation patterns. This provides accurate multi-step lookahead over future expert usage, allowing the runtime to organize expert movement and execution ahead of verification and improve both communication and execution efficiency. Under single-request inference, Specter achieves an average 1.50x (up to 1.88x) speedup in time per output token (TPOT) over the state-of-the-art system. Specter makes memory-constrained, single-GPU MoE inference more practical on commodity hardware. Our code is available at https://github.com/wwwwwf/specter_asplos."

Abstract EdgeKernelBench and LEKA enable unified evaluation and layered optimization of LLM-generated kernels across heterogeneous edge backends, revealing a gap between correctness and performance.  Generated by thinkingmachines/Inkling-Small Recent works show that LLMs can generate kernels with strong performance on server GPUs. Edge deployment presents distinct challenges because the hardware is heterogeneous, the software stacks are fragmented, and the resource budgets are tight. LLM kernel generation across diverse edge backends has yet to be studied systematically. We introduce EdgeKernelBench, which combines a unified IR deployment framework with a hierarchical benchmark for edge workloads. The unified IR captures reusable computation, scheduling, and memory decisions and connects to each target backend through a compiler interface. The benchmark spans progressively larger program scopes, from individual operators to compact model graphs, across representative edge application domains. We instantiate the design with TVM as a reference compiler and validate it across heterogeneous edge backends. With a single generation attempt, the best model achieves 64.5% Correct@1 but only 25.5% Fast@1.0. This gap shows that functional correctness does not consistently translate into performance gains. We therefore introduce the Layered Edge Kernel Agent (LEKA), which organizes optimization through a layered IR. On the agent evaluation subset, LEKA achieves 43.5% Fast@1.0, compared with 13.0% for direct generation and 26.1% for Claude Code. LEKA and Claude Code use the same base model and optimization budget. Together, EdgeKernelBench and LEKA support unified evaluation and layered optimization of kernels generated by LLMs across edge backends.
[21.10.2026 07:05] Response: {"desc": "Статья представляет EdgeKernelBench — единый фреймворк и иерархический бенчмарк для оценки ядер (kernels), которые генерируют LLM для разнородных edge-устройств с фрагментированным ПО и ограниченными ресурсами. В основе лежит унифицированное промежуточное представление (IR), которое через компилятор TVM связывает вычисления, планирование и управление памятью с разными аппаратными бэкендами. Эксперименты показывают разрыв между корректностью (Correct@1 = 64.5%) и производительностью (Fast@1.0 = 25.5%) сгенерированных ядер. Для его преодоления авторы предлагают LEKA — послойного агента оптимизации, который достигает 43.5% Fast@1.0 против 13.0% при прямой генерации и 26.1% у Claude Code.", "emoji": "⚡", "title": "Корректный код — это ещё не быстрый код: слоистая оптимизация ядер LLM для edge-устройств"}

Abstract This work introduces a spatio-temporal Painting Change Map to control video generation of artwork by tracking localized structural changes across time, enabling fine-grained guidance for painting and unpainting processes.  Generated by thinkingmachines/Inkling-Small Digital art generation has undergone a massive paradigm shift, moving from static image synthesis to dynamic video generation that can mimic the human painting process. While existing models can synthesize visually stunning time-lapses of paintings being created or "unpainted," they largely treat the creative process as a black box. Current conditioning mechanisms are either too rigid, relying on static geometric cues like Canny edges or depth maps, or too holistic, utilizing simple scalar progress variables that offer no localized control. Consequently, users lack the agency to steer how a painting evolves over time. In this work, we argue that true control over the video generation of artwork requires a joint understanding of two fundamental dimensions: where an artist acts, and when those actions take place. To achieve this, we introduce the Painting Change Map, a novel spatio-temporal control mechanism that explicitly tracks normalized structural changes across a 1,000-timestep continuum, a resolution sufficient to capture fine-grained stroke-level changes while remaining computationally tractable. By mapping localized visual differences into an encoded RGB space, our representation enables fine-grained guidance for both the forward painting and reverse unpainting processes. Extensive benchmarking demonstrates the efficacy of our approach. We outperform existing control baselines and achieve competitive or superior results compared to state-of-the-art painting methods. Furthermore, under oracle conditions, our method surpasses all baselines by a significant margin. Code is available at https://github.com/Markus-Pobitzer/painting-change-map.
[21.10.2026 07:05] Response: {"desc": "Авторы представляют Painting Change Map — пространственно-временной механизм управления для генерации видео картин, который отслеживает локальные структурные изменения на шкале из 1000 временных шагов. Карта изменений кодируется в RGB-пространство и подаётся как условие для модели генерации, что позволяет точно задавать процессы рисования и «стирания» картины по мелким мазкам. Такой подход превосходит существующие методы управления, основанные на Canny-границах, картах глубины или скалярном прогрессе, которые дают лишь грубый контроль. По результатам бенчмарков метод сравним или превосходит современные решения для генерации процесса живописи, а в условиях oracle-оценки значительно опережает все baseline-модели.", "emoji": "🎨", "title": "Управляй не только где, но и когда художник делает мазок"}

Extensive benchmarking demonstrates the efficacy of our approach. We outperform existing control baselines and achieve competitive or superior results compared to state-of-the-art painting methods. Furthermore, under oracle conditions, our method surpasses all baselines by a significant margin. Code is available at https://github.com/Markus-Pobitzer/painting-change-map."

"Abstract This work introduces a spatio-temporal Painting Change Map to control video generation of artwork by tracking localized structural changes across time, enabling fine-grained guidance for painting and unpainting processes.  Generated by thinkingmachines/Inkling-Small Digital art generation has undergone a massive paradigm shift, moving from static image synthesis to dynamic video generation that can mimic the human painting process. While existing models can synthesize visually stunning time-lapses of paintings being created or "unpainted," they largely treat the creative process as a black box. Current conditioning mechanisms are either too rigid, relying on static geometric cues like Canny edges or depth maps, or too holistic, utilizing simple scalar progress variables that offer no localized control. Consequently, users lack the agency to steer how a painting evolves over time. In this work, we argue that true control over the video generation of artwork requires a joint understanding of two fundamental dimensions: where an artist acts, and when those actions take place. To achieve this, we introduce the Painting Change Map, a novel spatio-temporal control mechanism that explicitly tracks normalized structural changes across a 1,000-timestep continuum, a resolution sufficient to capture fine-grained stroke-level changes while remaining computationally tractable. By mapping localized visual differences into an encoded RGB space, our representation enables fine-grained guidance for both the forward painting and reverse unpainting processes. Extensive benchmarking demonstrates the efficacy of our approach. We outperform existing control baselines and achieve competitive or superior results compared to state-of-the-art painting methods. Furthermore, under oracle conditions, our method surpasses all baselines by a significant margin. Code is available at https://github.com/Markus-Pobitzer/painting-change-map."

Abstract DecisionPivot is a diagnostic benchmark that evaluates decision models across controlled variations, revealing gaps between aggregate accuracy, contrast-group performance, calibration, and reasoning effort.  Generated by thinkingmachines/Inkling-Small Decision models support routing, guardrails, and output verification in LLM systems. However, item accuracy alone cannot establish whether models make correct decisions across controlled variations or assign reliable confidence to their predictions. We introduce DecisionPivot, a diagnostic benchmark comprising 48 tasks and 40,033 items across seven decision roles. Its core track contains 7,800 items from 13 generated task families, organized into contrast groups with computed answers verified by independently implemented solvers. The complementary reference track contains 32,233 items from 35 public test sets, preserving their original evaluation metrics. We evaluate 40 decision models and 14 general models using documented, model-specific protocols, assessing item and contrast-group accuracy, probability ordering, calibration, and empirical selective coverage. Our results reveal substantial differences obscured by aggregate scores: two 27B models separated by only 2.6 points on the reference track achieve contrast-group accuracies of 70.1% and 44.2%. Across 2,400 binary contrast pairs, Perplexity Decider correctly ranks the positive member above the negative member in 92.3% of pairs but answers both members correctly in only 74.1%, exposing a gap between probability ordering and decision correctness. Models with similar item accuracy also differ in the reliability of their high-confidence predictions. On a separate 1,296-item subset, GLM-5.3 achieves 93.5\% contrast-group accuracy at a higher reasoning setting, compared with 81.5% at its lowest effort. By linking errors to pivotal details and difficulty levels, DecisionPivot provides fine-grained diagnostics of decision failures that aggregate evaluation alone cannot reveal.

Основной трек содержит 7 800 элементов из 13 сгенерированных семейств задач, сгруппированных в контрастные группы, а справочный трек включает 32 233 элемента из 35 публичных тестовых наборов. Abstract HybridResearcher is a small agent trained via synthetic cross-source trajectories and reinforcement learning to reason across structured and unstructured data, outperforming much larger models without forgetting single-source skills.  Generated by thinkingmachines/Inkling-Small While LLM agents are increasingly capable of gathering and synthesizing evidence from a single homogeneous source, such as structured tables or unstructured data, real-world analytical questions frequently span heterogeneous sources. For example, answering one enterprise question may require both a result computed over an internal database and evidence found on the web. Recent evaluations on benchmarks like HybridDeepResearch show that even frontier agents struggle when evidence must cross this boundary. While these benchmarks have exposed the limitation, methodologies to explicitly teach cross-source reasoning remain absent. To address this, we introduce HybridResearcher, a 4B agent trained to bridge evidence across the structured and unstructured boundary. To build it, we synthesize cross-source bridge trajectories by starting with validated single-source tasks-such as existing text-to-SQL or web search queries-and fabricating the missing source around a bridge entity drawn from the seed's own output. We retain a sample only when strict construction gates confirm that neither side alone suffices, ensuring the data requires true cross-source interdependence. Finally, we train on this curated dataset using supervised fine-tuning followed by reinforcement learning. Evaluated on the existing HybridDeepResearch benchmark, HybridResearcher improves over our base model, Qwen3.5-4B, by 30.0 points pass@8 and surpasses the far larger Qwen3.5-397B-A17B by 8.3 points, while also improving over its base model on established, held-out single-source benchmarks (BrowseComp and BrowseComp-Plus), demonstrating that this complex cross-source reasoning can be taught directly to small models without triggering catastrophic forgetting.

Для обучения создаётся синтетический датасет кросс-источниковых траекторий: к проверенным однотипным задачам (например, text-to-SQL или веб-поиск) добавляется недостающий источник вокруг общей сущности, а строгие фильтры оставляют только примеры, где ни один источник не достаточен сам по себе. Модель дообучается сначала через supervised fine-tuning, а затем через reinforcement learning. While these benchmarks have exposed the limitation, methodologies to explicitly teach cross-source reasoning remain absent. To address this, we introduce HybridResearcher, a 4B agent trained to bridge evidence across the structured and unstructured boundary. To build it, we synthesize cross-source bridge trajectories by starting with validated single-source tasks-such as existing text-to-SQL or web search queries-and fabricating the missing source around a bridge entity drawn from the seed's own output. We retain a sample only when strict construction gates confirm that neither side alone suffices, ensuring the data requires true cross-source interdependence. Finally, we train on this curated dataset using supervised fine-tuning followed by reinforcement learning. Evaluated on the existing HybridDeepResearch benchmark, HybridResearcher improves over our base model, Qwen3.5-4B, by 30.0 points pass@8 and surpasses the far larger Qwen3.5-397B-A17B by 8.3 points, while also improving over its base model on established, held-out single-source benchmarks (BrowseComp and BrowseComp-Plus), demonstrating that this complex cross-source reasoning can be taught directly to small models without triggering catastrophic forgetting."

Abstract HarnessRL co-evolves a training harness with the policy via adaptive task sampling, rollout guidance, and rubric updates to prevent reward failure in open-ended reinforcement learning.  Generated by thinkingmachines/Inkling-Small Rubric-based reinforcement learning (RL) trains policies for open-ended generation with natural-language criteria as rewards, but these rewards can become ineffective as training progresses. We identify two failure modes. Under non-discriminative rewards, scores collapse to uniformly low or high values due to insufficient exploration or a mismatch between task difficulty and policy capability. Under spurious rewards, higher scores do not correspond to better responses. Both reflect a training configuration whose task sampling, exploration, and evaluation stay fixed as the policy evolves. We introduce HarnessRL, a training-time harness that co-evolves with the policy through three interfaces for task sampling, rollout guidance, and rubric criteria. HarnessRL maintains a training-time memory from which a harness evolver retrieves within-task history and cross-task evidence. Using this evidence, the evolver attributes each reward failure to the interface whose update can address it. It then runs a proposer and just-in-time critic loop in which the critic accepts a candidate update only after measuring its effect on the current frozen policy, returning rejected candidates for revision. We also curate Rubric-Mix-10k, a five-domain training and evaluation suite for open-ended RL across medical, science, writing, role-play, and instruction-following tasks. On Rubric-Mix-10k, HarnessRL outperforms the strongest baselines by 2.8 to 3.1 points across both 4B and 9B model scales and nearly halves the training steps to its best checkpoint. Code and data are available at https://github.com/Tongyi-ConvAI/Qwen-Character/tree/main/HarnessRL.
[21.10.2026 07:05] Response: {"desc": "В статье представлен HarnessRL — обучающий фреймворк, который совместно эволюционирует вместе с policy модели и управляет выборкой задач, guidance для rollout и критериями rubric в reinforcement learning. Авторы выделяют два типа сбоев наград: non-discriminative rewards, когда оценки схлопываются, и spurious rewards, когда высокий балл не соответствует качеству ответа. Для их устранения используется память обучения, harness evolver и цикл proposer и just-in-time critic, который принимает обновления только после проверки их эффекта на замороженной модели. На новом наборе Rubric-Mix-10k метод превосходит сильнейшие baseline на 2.8–3.1 пункта для моделей 4B и 9B и почти вдвое сокращает число шагов обучения.", "emoji": "🧭", "title": "Обучающая обвязка, которая растёт вместе с моделью, спасает reward от поломок"}
